| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.certify | replace_bounds | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | Per-sample two-region bounds of replace(f, c) for every row f with a single cover in the sample (index >= 0) and every path c of one group: out[s, i, j] for the j-th such row of sample s (rows in increasing order). The paths are given as their distinct prefixes (blocks < split; sorted; prefix_common = the leading blocks shared with the previous prefix) and distinct suffixes (blocks >= split; sorted from the last block; suffix_common = the trailing blocks shared with the previous suffix). The DP is max-plus linear in its state, so a path's bound is max_j F[j] + beta[j] for the forward state F after its prefix and the backward vector beta of its suffix: every distinct prefix and suffix is run once, reusing the state over shared leading (trailing) blocks. Equal to _two_region's recursion in exact arithmetic; float rounding differs by the summation order. Only the rows with wanted[f] are bounded: each row's DP is independent of the others, so their values are unchanged, and the other rows get -inf (their replacements are not searched). Emissions: an EmissionLayout's arrays. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.certify | single_exclusions | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, int64, Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | G^{-f} for every (sample, f) with index >= 0, and the single covers of each such forward (second[s, f, j]: j covers a maximiser set of P \ f). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.certify | structural_bounds | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, int64, Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | Per-sample two-region bounds of the local, onesided and splice moves (samples x columns). Each value is move_bounds' recursion evaluated in two halves: the DP is max-plus linear in its state, so a move's bound is max_j (state before the part where the move differs from a reference)[j] + (backward vector after that part)[j], equal in exact arithmetic (float rounding differs by the summation order only). Per founder f, one forward and one backward pass with new path f give the prefix states and suffix vectors of every local move of f (it differs from f at one block only, which is then advanced from the prefix state); one backward pass per ordered pair (f, g) with new path g gives the suffixes of every onesided move f[:t] + g[t:] (its prefix is f's); one forward and one backward pass per unordered pair give every splice. Regions G^{-F} are chosen as in move_bounds (region_source). local_moves rows: (column, founder, block, new local row); onesided_table[f, g, t] and splice_table[f, g, t] (f < g): column of that move or -1; active[f]: f has local or onesided moves here. O(m k (k^2 + sum_b K_b)) per sample instead of move_bounds' O(m k B (k^2 + sum_b K_b)). |

At a complete site only the three dosage codes occur, so only their logs are evaluated; the values are the same as with all seven (each code's log is computed alone). |

The primary recurrence is the ordinary join score, without a traceback band. Counts break exact score ties only; the stored representative prior never changes its LL. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.objective | paint | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, int64, int64, float64, bool)` | True | Viterbi paintings of samples start .. stop - 1: per bin the state a * K + c (a <= c) of the maximising sequence (the traceback of :func:`forward`'s recursion; same values). Tie rules within the band b = relative x max(1, /the bin's best score/) (relative 0 uses exact comparisons): the switch source is the first state in pair order within b of the maximum, a state stays when stay >= switch - b, the last bin's state is the first within b of the maximum. The recursion's values stay exact maxima; a traceback choice inside the band costs the painted sequence at most b, and near-equal sequences (equal scores reached through different summation orders, which a tiny input perturbation reorders) are painted by the content rule (stay, then the lower pair, the panel being in canonical order; under the convention reversal ``reverse`` the last state in pair order instead of the first, "stay" being a stated prior). int32[stop - start, total bins]. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.proposals | _possibly_novel | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | True where some sample's block-restart bound exceeds margin[s]. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.proposals | _stream_static_gains | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | Accumulate blocks before mate maxima; retain only a K-vector per task. Candidates are visited in sorted order (order, common = leading blocks shared with the previous one) in contiguous chunks, one task each: the block sums of a shared prefix are reused, accumulated in block order; per candidate the samples are summed in order. Each task sums into rows of its own (the same chain per cell, from zero) and copies them out at the end: rows of tasks interleaved in candidate order would share cache lines. Block b's totals use packed unordered pairs: flat[start[b] + sample * P_b + pair_index(a, c)]. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.proposals | block_restart_scores | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, int64)` | True | W[s, b, r]: prefix_scores' recursion over block b alone for candidate row r, started afresh at the block's first bin (-inf for rows a block does not have). Any state sequence restricted to one block is a sequence of that block alone, and the switches at block starts are free here, so sum_b W[s, b, c_b] >= the candidate's score. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.proposals | conditional_fields | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int32, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'A', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)` | True | Per-path replacement deltas and pair correction for a suffix exchange. Use the same emission values as the float64 full-score tensor. Homozygous painting states replace BOTH copies once, rather than treating them as two independent replacements. Uniform/masked input has zero deltas. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.proposals | fixed_paint_delta | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int32, 2, 'A', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'A', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'A', False, aligned=True), int64)` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.splice | _junction_scores | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.join.splice | score_splices | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, int64)` | True | Per-sample score of every suffix exchange: (S, B-1, K(K-1)/2). Boundary index b-1 exchanges rows on blocks b..B-1; pair columns follow pair_column. Rows retain their slots, including duplicates during seed refits. The caller performs the ordinary admissibility checks before using a move. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.linker.emissions | log_emissions | `(Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, int64)` | True | float32[S, L, codes] log emission of every sample, site and pair class (the linker's observation model). likelihoods: float32[S, L, 3] genotype likelihoods of the batch's informative samples (the neutral view: exactly 1/3 where a sample is unobserved; not renormalised). robustness r: each class's predictive likelihood is mixed with a uniform genotype, (1 - r) P + r / 3, then bounded below by LINKER_EMISSION_FLOOR before taking the log. The mixture keeps incompatible observations finite while preserving their evidence. codes: 3 for a fully called block, 7 otherwise (the first three classes are identical in both). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.linker.emissions | pair_codes | `(Array(int8, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int32, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | uint8[L, K * K] code of every ordered founder pair (a, b) at column a * K + b of each site. alleles: int8[K, L] pre-fill founder alleles (-1 = unknown). source_rows: int[K, L] the latent row each founder copies at each site (equal rows share an unknown allele). |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.linker.kernels | _log_sample_counts | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.linker.kernels | edge_log_counts | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | float64[K_l, K_r] log posterior homologue edge counts of one boundary, summed over samples. s: float64[S, K_l^2] forward scores of the left block, r: float64[S, K_r^2] backward scores of the right block, log_t: float64[K_l, K_r] the log transitions both were computed with. For each sample, with A, B the boundary likelihood matrices, W1 = A T B^T, W2 = A^T T B, Z = sum(T * W1), the expected counts T * (W1 + W2) / Z count both homologues (two per informative sample), without assuming swap symmetry or square T. prange partitions samples, then output edges: no reduction crosses workers. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.refinement.acceptance | _relabel_chain | `(Chain(bool, int64, int64, array(float64, 3d, C), array(float64, 3d, C), array(float64, 2d, C)), int64, Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), bool)` | True | The backward chain after the suffix exchange perm at junction p: on every block from p on two founders' rows are swapped, so state q there holds the pair dosages of the former state perm[q]; the backward recursion is the same up to that state order (its maxima and lexicographic maxima are order-free), so the exact messages and counts at boundaries >= p are the former ones relabelled and the block normalisers are unchanged. Boundaries left of p are dropped. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.refinement.component_workspace | binned_emissions | `(Array(float32, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), ListType(Array(int8, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True)), ListType(Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, ListType(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True)))` | True | Binned surrogate emissions of every block and pair of its local rows, into the zeroed outputs[b] float64 (S, R_b(R_b + 1)/2, bins_b) (symmetric in the two rows). The model of the join's binned scorer: per kept site with informative evidence the robust emission max(log(0.99 P + 0.01/3), -2) - log(1/3), P the pair's genotype likelihood (hard dosage when every row of the block is called at its kept sites, else the predictive pair codes with unknown alleles marginalised; prepared panels are atomic, so only a row paired with itself shares an unknown allele), summed over the sites of each bin of bin_size block sites. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.refinement.count | _family_forward | `(Array(int16, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Tuple((Array(float32, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), bool)), int64, int64, Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int16, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | Raw max-path scores of every member of a factored shadow family, sharing prefixes. base: int16 (K, L) coded alleles of the family's first member; unit u (sorted, disjoint site ranges [unit_start[u], unit_stop[u])) has n_u = unit_offsets[u + 1] - unit_offsets[u] alternative allele slabs unit_alleles[unit_offsets[u] + c] (K x width_u used). out: float64 (s1 - s0, M), M = prod n_u members in depth-first order (unit 0 slowest), for the samples [s0, s1) (the window should hold every site of them: a sample's depth-first walk revisits the whole component). A sample's forward row at a site depends only on the alleles before it, so a member shares the row of its common prefix bit for bit: every member's score equals score_panel's on its full allele matrix (the same site steps in the same order). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.refinement.count | _family_sites |  | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.refinement.count | option_scores | `(ListType(Array(int16, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Tuple((Array(float32, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), bool)), int64)` | True | Optimistic per-sample block scores for the deletion round's summed bound. sigma[o] scores option o; option_offsets bounds each block's options. A block with target + 1 rows has one option per removed row, otherwise one containing every local row. A panel fixes one option per block for all samples together. Each sample's chain starts afresh at every block, using the target K's switch costs. Normalisers are added by the caller. Blocks of a stretch run in parallel. |

per-sample F gains (count x S) of one-founder content moves. Candidate i replaces the rows of founder focal[i] on blocks [first_block[i], end_block[i]) (rows[row_offsets[i]:row_offsets[i + 1]]); the current panel (haps) is unchanged elsewhere. With f_start[i] / g_start[i] the current normalised forward / backward messages at first_block[i], g_end[i] the current backward message at end_block[i] and scale_sum[i] the backward normalisers of those blocks, the gain of sample s is max_q [f_start (x) T_new (x) g_end] + new normalisers - max_q [f_start + g_start] - scale_sum. The all-state recursion runs on per-segment dosage tables (item_dosages; a window candidate is normally one segment; a segment holds at most the window's capacity of sites and is covered before it is read); each sample's state carries from segment to segment. Candidates one after another, their samples in parallel. |

aligned=True), int64, int64, int64, Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), bool)` | True | Switch events of each listed sample's lexicographic maximum path on blocks [a, c). The path is the traceback of the accumulators of messages_lex (maximum score, then the lowest switch cost among exact maxima) started from the current messages f_start / fcnt_start at boundary a and closed by g_end / gcnt_end at boundary c (one row per listed sample). With focal >= 0 founder focal's rows are replaced on blocks [p1, p2) by `rows`. Per site and state the traceback keeps the route taken (stay; one homologue from the founder maximum R[x] of the state's first or second founder, whose partner is recorded; both from the overall maximum), first state among exact ties (last under the convention reversal). Returns the path's state at boundaries a and c, its whole switch cost (prefix and suffix costs from the messages), its number of events inside [a, c), the events (site, state before, state after) and their costs (c at the site per changed homologue). The window covers [a, c) first (a longer stretch is read beyond its capacity through the exact fallback). |

A block's forward step reads only the message at its start and the block's alleles, so a sample's messages over an unchanged block whose start message is unchanged are the earlier ones. Per sample, a block is recomputed (messages()' recursion from the stored start message) only if it changed or its start message did; a recomputed end message that equals the earlier one ends the run. Likewise backwards. Bitwise messages() of the current panel. Only the segments it recomputes are covered. |

Bitwise messages_lex() of the current panel. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.assembly.refinement.proposals | gather_macro | `(ListType(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), ListType(Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)))`<br>`(ListType(Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True)), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), ListType(Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)))` | True | Coarse-unit emissions: for L1 span groups[g] = (first, end) prepared blocks and its alphabet rows_by_group[g] (A_g row tuples over the span), out[g][s, pair_index(a, b), :] concatenates the blocks' binned emissions of the row pairs (rows[a, block], rows[b, block]). One task per group, which reads each source block of its group from the typed List once and copies it for every sample: every List element read updates a reference count shared by all threads, including the window workers that gather their problems concurrently from the component's one List, so reads per (group, sample, block) serialised them. O(N sum_g A_g^2 B_g) time for group alphabet A_g and bin count B_g. |

With a_g = log_probs[s, l, g], a row-pair state (i, j) emits at a site a0 + (a1 - a0)(H_i + H_j) + (a0 - 2 a1 + a2) H_i H_j, and a row-unknown state (k, U) emits m01 + (m12 - m01) H_k - lambda (m01 = max(a0, a1), m12 = max(a1, a2); lambda only at observed cells). Per (sample, bin): C0b = sum a0, kW_Cb = sum m01 - lambda x observed sites; per (bin, site in bin, sample): diff1 = a1 - a0, w = a0 - 2 a1 + a2, kWdiff = m12 - m01 (zero beyond the last site). |

Cov(pi)-Cov(r) has off-diagonal pair terms r_i*r_j-pi_i*pi_j. The binomial kernels and posterior weights are exactly those of the objective. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.hmm | _minimum_switch_counts | `(Array(int32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True),)` | True |  |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.hmm | canonical_track_order | `(Array(int64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int32, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | bool[N, B]: whether to exchange the two tracks of each bin, so that the track order (the H1/H2 gauge) is a function of the unordered candidate pairs alone. The unordered pair of candidate masks of a bin does not depend on the traceback convention (:func:`combine_candidate_masks`); the order read from one convention's Viterbi path did (its final state and traceback run from opposite ends). Right to left, as the default traceback: the last bin puts the smaller mask on track 0; every earlier bin takes the orientation with fewer track changes towards the bin after it (a track keeps its identity while consecutive masks share a state), then fewer allele-visible changes, and when both orientations are equal, the smaller mask on track 0. Unordered states, posteriors, switch counts and releases are unchanged. O(N B) for single-state masks (O(N B S^2) at worst). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.hmm | combine_candidate_masks | `(Array(int64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | int64[N, 2, B]: per bin the smallest unordered cover of two decodings' candidate sets, in ``primary``'s track order. ``primary`` and ``secondary`` are the candidate masks of two co-optimal Viterbi paths (the default and the mirrored traceback conventions, each with its switch intervals). Per bin the two track matchings give the covers (P0 / S0, P1 / S1) and (P0 / S1, P1 / S0); the one with fewer candidates is released (a pure track swap of the secondary path costs nothing). When both are equally small, they are the same cover if they are equal as unordered pairs, and otherwise both tracks get every candidate. Symmetric in the two decodings up to the track order, so the released unordered candidate pairs do not depend on which convention is primary. O(N B S). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.hmm | direct_support | `(Array(int32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)`<br>`(Array(int32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64)` | True | bool[N, 2, B]: the track's Viterbi class is called at an informative site of the bin. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.hmm | hamming_max_values | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, float64, float64, int64, Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.hmm | parsimonious_track_order | `(Array(int32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int32, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | Among equal-score Viterbi paths, the track order with fewer allele changes. Emissions are symmetric in track order and the Hamming transition is invariant to swapping both tracks, so swapping the whole path suffix after boundary t-1/t changes the score only through that transition: when the ordered and swapped continuations change equally many tracks, both are exact Viterbi paths. At such ties the suffix is swapped iff the swapped order has strictly fewer allele-visible track changes (a change between rows sharing a local key < n_classes in bin t-1 or t is invisible). Unordered states, and hence every posterior summary, are unchanged. O(N B). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.hmm | switch_interval_masks | `(Array(int32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)`<br>`(Array(int32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)`<br>`(Array(int32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64)`<br>`(Array(int32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64)` | True | int64[N, 2, B] candidate states of each track and bin as bit masks. A track change a -> b of the (gauge-fixed) Viterbi path at the edge after bin e can sit at any edge of the maximal run of bins around e in which a and b are indistinguishable for the sample (paired with the other track's state, no observed site of the bin where their genotype laws differ, :func:`_distinguishable`): the data cannot place the switch inside the run (its emissions are equal for both), so only the transition prior places the switch there, and the traceback convention breaks exact ties. The bins of that run get both a and b as candidates; elsewhere the candidate is the Viterbi state. A run stops at the track's neighbouring switches (each switch keeps one bin of its own segments), so it never removes a switch. Exact discrete test, O(N L). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.label_continuity | _add_transposition_gains | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | Add the exact Viterbi-score gain of each (link, transposition) of the eligible samples of ``left``/``right`` to total[link, pair], sample by sample in index order (so batches added in sample order give the same sums as all samples at once). Unaffected states keep their identity terms, so per (link, sample) the terms are sorted once and each pair takes the first sorted entry outside its rows and columns, then evaluates only its 4S - 4 affected states. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.label_continuity | _centre | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True),)` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.label_continuity | _link_messages | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)`<br>`(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64, Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | Max-product forward messages into, and backward messages from, chosen bins. forward[s, k] = max over paths through bins < b of their score plus the transition into each state of b (zero for the first bin); backward[s, k] = max over continuations after b of their transitions and emissions. Both exclude bin b's own emission and are centred per bin; the centring constants cancel in every gain because one link combines one forward and one backward message for the identity and every relabelling alike. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.label_continuity | _link_scores | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | Per link: left = forward + bin parts left of the link; right = parts at or after it + backward. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.label_continuity | _relabelled_emissions | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | Bin emissions of the current labelling, for ``samples``, from emissions on bin parts. state_maps[segment, x] is the original state whose content the current state x carries in that segment; a bin straddling a link sums its parts. Each row is computed from its own sample alone, in the same order. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.painting.label_continuity | _stacked_relabelled_emissions | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | True | _relabelled_emissions under each of several labellings at once: row g * len(samples) + r holds sample samples[r] under state_maps[g], each computed from its own sample alone, in the same order. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.pedigree.family | _cavity_iterations | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64, float64, float64, int64, int64, float64, int64)` | True | Iterations ``first`` .. ``stop - 1`` of the converged cavity solve (see _converged_cavity), resumable. Returns (messages, iterations done, residual, status): status 1 = converged (the messages are the converged odds), 2 = paused, 0 = ``stop`` reached (the messages are the damped state to continue from). ``history`` (the pause rule's residual ring) is updated in place. ``previous`` is read, never written. Each iteration fuses incoming-message adjustment, family log odds and the cavity update in one parallel pass (_child_cavity), reusing buffers. The residual is a maximum of non-negative changes, which any grouping returns exactly; a NaN change triggers a serial fold in child/parent slot order because NaN handling depends on order. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.pedigree.family | _exp_or_zero | `(float64,)` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.pedigree.family | _family_log_odds | `(Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)` | True | Included/excluded log odds of every [child, parent] in O(A + N^2), stably. | haplotype_reconstruction.science.pedigree.forward | _fill_named_first_products | `(Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, int64)`<br>`(Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, int64)` | True | Complete, in place, the cached products that no candidate changes when the first slot transmits a named state and the second slot is the child's null source (M0/M1 configurations). A named state passes through every candidate's projection unchanged (projection._transmitted_alt), so under M0 and M1 the entries with a named first state depend on the child, bin and state pair only, also where the second state is BACKGROUND or a named allele is uncalled (which _common_products leaves NaN). Each such entry is computed exactly as score_path's general path computes it (site order, likelihood, the zero-likelihood rule); an entry that path would find unstable (underflow) stays NaN, so the general path still meets it and switches to log space. The powered (tempered) cache is not extended: those entries keep their direct power. Not valid for M2, whose second slot is a candidate's. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.pedigree.forward | _powered_products | `(Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)`<br>`(Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64)` | True | Cache normalized powers, plus their exact normalization maximum. The final row stores the maximum in column zero. A consumer may reuse an entry only when its complete emission matrix has exactly that maximum; otherwise it evaluates the same normalized power directly. Unknown products stay NaN. This makes cache capacity and completion arithmetically neutral. |

Only BACKGROUND (no panel haplotype) is conditioned on the candidate's reads. A named class transmits its panel allele, or BACKGROUND's ALT frequency where the panel left it uncalled: the panel leaves alleles uncalled where the data are noisy, and there the candidate's own GL rows are noisy too (simulations: heterozygous- looking at ~40% of truly homozygous such sites), so conditioning on them cost true parents tens of nats per chromosome against M0 and the relative models. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.refit.refit | _flip_gain | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 1, 'A', False, aligned=True), int64)`<br>`(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64)` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.refit.refit | _painted_together | `(Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.refit.refit | _pair_curvature | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 1, 'A', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)`<br>`(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int8, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.refit.refit | _refit_kernel | `(Array(int8, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int16, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int16, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, float64, int64, bool, int64)`<br>`(Array(int8, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int16, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int16, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, float64, int64, bool, int64)` | True | Coordinate ascent per site on a founder-pair log-likelihood table. ``table[a, b, g]`` (a <= b) sums log((1 - eps) P(reads / dosage g) + eps / 3) over observed samples painted with the unordered pair {a, b}; a row's objective then needs only its partners' current alleles. ``favour[a, b]`` counts those samples whose own reads favour one dosage over the next (a < b: 1 over 0, 0 over 1, 2 over 1, 1 over 2; a == b: 2 over 0, 0 over 2) by more than the tie quantum, the supporters of a fill (module docstring). Pairs whose partner allele is unknown contribute nothing, as do unpainted tracks. Each sweep tries the single-row flips (accepted at a gain >= minimum_gain), then the swaps of two rows with different alleles that are painted together at the site (``together[a, b]``: some sample painted {a, b} is observed there), accepted at a gain >= 2 x minimum_gain because it changes two cells; moves are judged against the round's start state in decision order (module docstring). Per site (finite tables): O(N) to fill the table, then per round O(K^2) for the K flip gains and the O(1) two-row gains of the P co-painted pairs (P <= min(N, K (K - 1) / 2)) and O(n) to order n qualifying moves; a round applies a move or releases alleles, so a phase has at most K rounds per sweep (and K/2 releasing rounds per site). Conflict tests: flip phase O(K^2) per round; two-row phase O(K^2 + P) per round for certified classes plus the tests of uncertified members (_swap_conflicts_fast). Total O(N + (S + 1) K^3 + K sum_r Uu_r) per site over S sweeps (Uu_r: uncertified unshared tied members of two-row round r; _swap_conflicts_fast), cubic when every tied member is certified, O(N + K^4) per sweep at worst; tables with an infinite entry use direct O(K) gains and tests (up to O(K^5) in one round). Fills add O(K^2) per site. SITE_TILE-site tiles in parallel. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | alone_logliks | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[c] = family_loglik(C[c], C[c], 0.0, logpi, logpi) over all sites: sample c alone with latent parents. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | family_loglik | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Literal[int](0), int64)`<br>`(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | family_loglik_parallel | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)` | True | family_loglik with its sites' terms evaluated in parallel and summed in site order (the same value). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | family_logliks | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64, Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[j] = family_loglik of family families[j] with its slot laws, over sites [lo, hi). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | family_screen | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | h[i] = max_k (Au[i, gu] + Av[i, gv] + S_f[i, k]) and H[i, k] = exp(Au + Av + S_f - h[i]) (k = 3 gu + gv): a family's terms with its parent laws in the linear domain, for the join screen. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | full_sib_gains | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[t] = the latent full-sib merge gain of families first[t] < second[t] (pair_gains' first column). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | half_screen | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[t] = the screen's estimate of the half-sib link gain of families f = first[t], g = second[t]: base[f] + base[g] + sum_i log sum_gu pi(gu) Y[f, i, gu] Y[g, i, gu] (base[f] = sum_i y[f, i] - ll[f]). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | half_sib_gains | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[t] = half_sib_loglik(S[f], S[g]) - ll[f] - ll[g] over all sites, (f, g) = rows[t]. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | half_sib_loglik | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Literal[int](0), int64)`<br>`(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)` | False | Serial scalar/array kernel |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | join_entries | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[t] = family_loglik(S[f] + C[c], f's slot laws) - ll[f] over all sites, f = families[t], c = samples[t] (join_gains for one sample). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | join_screen | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[c] = the screen's estimate of join_entries' family_loglik(S_f + C[c], Au, Av) - ll_f for every sample c: base + nsum[c] + sum_i log sum_k H[i, k] G[c, i, k] (base = sum_i h[i] - ll_f, nsum[c] = sum_i n[c, i]). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | latent_alternatives | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64, Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[j, k] = family_loglik of family f = families[j] over sites [lo, hi) with slot k latent and the other slot as it is (NaN for a latent slot k): the alternative a sequenced parent must beat. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | leave_entries | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[t] = the change of family f = families[t] when sample c = samples[t] leaves it: -ll[f] for a one-member family (single[t]), else family_loglik(S[f] - C[c], f's slot laws) - ll[f]. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | member_parent_logliks | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[j] = family_loglik(S_f - C[p], Au, logpost[p]) over all sites, p = members[j]: the family without member p and with p in its free parent slot. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | merge_gains | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | pair_gains for the merge step: out[t, 0] the full-sib merge gain; out[t, 1] the half-sib link gain, evaluated only when both merged slots are latent and the full-sib gain exceeds ``tolerance`` (NaN otherwise: such a pair is no merge offer either way). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | posterior_screen | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[p, i, g] = exp(logpost[p, i, g]) in float32: each sample's genotype posterior, for the slot screen. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | sample_screen | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | n[c, i] = max_k C[c, i, k] and G[c, i, k] = exp(C[c, i, k] - n[c, i]) (float32): each sample's child terms in the linear domain, for the join screen. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | screen_all | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64, Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out in packed upper-triangle order (f < g < n): the screen's latent full-sib merge gain estimate, base[f] + base[g] + sum_i log sum_k A[f, i, k] A[g, i, k] (base[f] = sum_i m[f, i] - ll[f]). Tiles of tile x tile families, each tile's rows read once, sites in order. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | screen_one | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[j] = the screen's estimate of the latent full-sib merge gain of family f and family others[j]. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | screen_shapes | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | For each family f in families (latent slots): m[f, i] = max_k (S[f][i, k] + W2[i, k]) and A[f, i, k] = exp(S[f][i, k] + W2[i, k] - m[f, i]) (W2 half the latent pair prior: the full-sib merge screen); y[f, i] = max_k S[f][i, k] and Y[f, i, gu] = sum_gv pi(gv) exp(S[f][i, 3 gu + gv] - y[f, i]) (the half-sib screen). A and Y in float32. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | slot_gains | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[p] = family_loglik(S_f, Au, logpost[p]) over all sites for each allowed sample p (NaN otherwise): the family with its free parent slot filled by sequenced sample p. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | slot_screen | `(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float32, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | out[p] = the screen's estimate of slot_gains' family_loglik(S_f, S_f, 0, Au, logpost[p]) for every sample p: sum_i [r_i + log sum_gv posterior[p, i, gv] R[i, gv]], R[i, gv] = sum_gu exp(Au[i, gu] + S_f[i, 3 gu + gv] - r_i) (r_i the largest exponent). |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.family_kernels | unit_terms | `(ListType(Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True)), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64, Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | One support unit (sites [lo, hi)): joins[j, c] = family_loglik(S[f] + C[c], f's laws) - base[j] for every live family f = families[j] and sample c; rest[c] = family_loglik(S[f] - C[c], f's laws) for c's own family f = families[family_of[c]] (0 for a one-member family, set by the caller); alone[c] = c alone with latent parents. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.frequency | allele_frequency_sites | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, float64, float64, float64, Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))`<br>`(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64, float64, float64, float64, Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))`<br>`(Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, float64, float64, float64, Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))`<br>`(Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64, float64, float64, float64, Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | allele_frequency per site: out[l] after ``iterations`` EM steps from 0.5 (posterior sums over the samples in sample order; a cell's genotype weights are summed in genotype order). |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.relatedness.ibd_kernels | pair_ibd | `(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))`<br>`(Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'F', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True))` | True | Pairwise 3-state IBD chain (IBD0, IBD1, IBD2; Jacquard emissions at allele frequency p under Hardy-Weinberg; sites not observed in both samples emit 1) for each pair (pairs[t, 0], pairs[t, 1]) over one chromosome's sites. stay[l]: the probability that the chain keeps its state from site l - 1 to l (exp(-rate x Morgans)); a leaving chain moves to the other allowed states equally. Accumulates posterior[t, 0:3] += the posterior state sums over jointly observed sites, posterior[t, 3] += their count, and loglik[t, m] += the forward log-likelihood with only the states allowed by masks[m]. Genotype likelihoods need no normalisation: every state of a pair uses the same reads. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.selection.context_refit | _carrier_weights | `(Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, float64, float64)`<br>`(Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), float64, float64, float64)` | True | Per sample and bin, the normalised cavity weights (bin emission excluded) of the ordered state pairs (forward-backward over bins, each step normalised), written over ``weights``, which holds the summed log emissions on entry (a sample's are read before its cavity weights replace them, so one (N, B, S, S) array serves both). Returns per sample and bin the MAP number of background copies (0, 1, 2): the smallest count among state pairs whose posterior weight is within ``count_band`` times the largest weight (the conservative side). |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.selection.path_model | _evaluate_lanes | `(Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64, int64)`<br>`(Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64, int64)`<br>`(Array(int64, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 4, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64, int64)` | True | Per-sample log likelihoods and the sufficient statistics at ``level``, each lane block's forward pass followed at once by its backward pass over the trajectory just stored in its worker's slot of pt / wt (``workers`` slots, not one per lane block). Totals add the samples in ascending order: with one worker the blocks run in order and add their lanes to the totals directly; otherwise waves of ``workers`` blocks run in parallel and store per-lane statistics, and every total element then adds the wave's lanes one by one in sample order. Every sum is therefore the same sequential chain over samples 0..N-1 for any thread count: a sample's +0 at an unobserved site adds nothing, as the totals start at +0 and so never hold -0. The statistics of a sample with likelihood -inf are meaningless; callers refuse such a panel. |

| haplotype_reconstruction.science.selection.path_model | _release_samples | `(Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), float64, float64, int64, bool)`<br>`(Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), float64, float64, int64, bool)`<br>`(Array(int64, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 3, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(bool, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), float64, float64, int64, bool)` | True | Per-sample posteriors for the release, aggregates summed in sample order. Returns per-sample log likelihoods; the exact one-bit gains summed over samples (K, L) (log of the marginal likelihood ratio of flipping founder a's allele at the site, every other bit and pi fixed; 0 at unobserved cells); per founder and site the favourable carriers by count (samples whose own exact gain is below -favourable_margin and whose carrier probability, the posterior probability that a homologue copies founder a with a homozygous state counted once, exceeds 1/2) and by mass (their summed carrier probabilities); copy occupancy summed over samples (S, L); per sample the largest MAP unknown-copy count (0, 1, 2) over its observed sites, where a larger count must win by more than ``count_band`` relative posterior mass (else the smaller count), with per sample the number of observed sites where the band took a smaller count than the strict MAP and the smallest relative gap between a site's two largest count masses (inf without observed sites); per sample the number of observed carrier cells (carrier probability above 1/2) whose exact gain lies inside the favourable band [-margin, margin] (the band around the exact zero of symmetric-evidence cells; not favourable there); and, with ``keep_gains`` (diagnostics only), every carrier cell's exact gain (N, K, L; NaN elsewhere), else a (1, 1, 1) placeholder. |
| haplotype_reconstruction.science.selection.path_model | _score_lanes | `(Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), int64, int64)`<br>`(Array(int64, 2, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64, int64)`<br>`(Array(int64, 2, 'C', True, aligned=True), Array(float64, 5, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', False, aligned=True), Array(float64, 1, 'C', True, aligned=True), int64, int64)` | True | Per-sample log likelihoods without trajectories: the same forward recursion, each worker keeping one prior and two row marginals. |
